Contraceptive discontinuation in the Democratic Republic of the Congo

DRC DHS 2023–24: national and provincial analysis, post-ending pathways, current users, and illustrative 2026 mCPR.

Modern-method discontinuation

Discontinuation rates use contraceptive-use episodes as their unit. National and provincial modern-method analyses include injectable, implant, pill, IUD, and other continuing modern-method episodes; emergency contraception is excluded from ongoing-method discontinuation risk. The survey mCPR includes all women aged 15–49 who report current modern use under the DHS definition. A 12-month cause-specific probability is a competing-risk life-table estimate, not the number of observed endings divided by the number of episodes.

Potentially actionable reasons: method failure; wanting a more effective method; bleeding; other side effects or health concerns; and access, cost, or inconvenience. Pregnancy intentions, partner disapproval, and circumstantial or unresolved reasons are competing endings rather than actionable events.
Rates use modern-method episodes; current-use projections use women aged 15–49. Emergency contraception appears in current mCPR but is excluded as an ongoing method at risk of discontinuation.

EC-inclusive initiation diagnostic

The denominator for a method-specific discontinuation probability is episodes that started that method. The initiation mix below uses all observed contraceptive starts, including non-modern methods and EC.

Weighting and survey design

Start shares use the DHS woman survey weight. Confidence intervals elsewhere account for stratification and clustered primary sampling units.

Discontinuation by time

Among all modern-method episodes, the 12-month probability of ending the original method for any recorded reason is 50.1%; the probability for potentially actionable reasons is 23.6%.

DRC DHS Table 7.13: reasons for discontinuation

Recorded reasons are mutually exclusive competing endings. A reason-specific 12-month risk uses all eligible episodes of that method at the start of the life table, with other endings treated as competing events.

Standardized current-user stock-to-outcome pathway

The current-user bridge starts with the projected number using a continuing modern method, excluding EC. It conditions the next 12-month actionable-ending risk on time already spent using that method and then distinguishes an original-method ending from leaving modern use at year end. The flow diagram shows only the subset projected to have an actionable ending; uninterrupted users and endings for other reasons are outside this flow.

Methods

Discontinuation rates use contraceptive-use episodes as their unit. National and provincial modern-method analyses include injectable, implant, pill, IUD, and other continuing modern-method episodes; emergency contraception is excluded from ongoing-method discontinuation risk. The survey mCPR includes all women aged 15–49 who report current modern use under the DHS definition. A 12-month cause-specific probability is a competing-risk life-table estimate, not the number of observed endings divided by the number of episodes.

Potentially actionable reasons: method failure; wanting a more effective method; bleeding; other side effects or health concerns; and access, cost, or inconvenience. Pregnancy intentions, partner disapproval, and circumstantial or unresolved reasons are competing endings rather than actionable events.

Survey weights and clustered, stratified variance estimation are used where confidence intervals or model tests are reported. Province-by-method-by-reason cells with few observed endings are flagged and should not be ranked.

Validation

The calendar reconstruction matches every published weighted episode count in Table 7.13; 104 of 110 rate cells agree to one decimal, with the remaining six differing by 0.1 percentage point.

Additional analyses

Program decisions can use the sequence: identify where actionable risk is high, inspect the reasons and method mix, estimate how many current users are affected, and compare realistic continuation scenarios. The mCPR illustrations are descriptive ceilings rather than causal program effects.

What is inside “Other” discontinuation reasons?

“Other fertility” comprises infrequent sex or husband away, marital dissolution or separation, and difficulty becoming pregnant or menopause. “Other recorded” contains fatalistic, other, unknown, missing, or unclassified responses.

Do distinct high-, medium-, and low-risk profiles emerge?

The full selected-reason competing-risk model predicts each observed-start episode’s 12-month discontinuation probability using its own method, province, and measured characteristics. Weighted tertiles form lower, middle, and higher predicted-risk bands. These bands are exploratory model summaries, not latent clusters or causal groups.

The detailed tables immediately below are the earlier 2024-to-2025 illustration. The 2026 duration-aware scenarios are shown later using a consistent 2026 population denominator.

First-year risks apply to new method starts. For users already on a method, the historical approach averages observed next-12-month probabilities at each method and elapsed-duration landmark over the current-user duration distribution. The AFT approach fits cause-specific accelerated failure-time models and predicts the next 12 months conditional on elapsed use. Both pathway-adjusted illustrations then use observed modern-use status after an actionable ending.

Unadjusted province pairwise tests

Pairwise differences were estimated from the stratified-PSU bootstrap of competing-risk rates and tested with two-sided normal approximations using bootstrap standard errors. Holm correction covers all 325 province pairs separately at 3 and 12 months. These are unadjusted comparisons; small-cell flags and uncertainty remain important.

Method starts and actionable risk, side by side

Gold bars show the method’s share of all method starts in a province. Navy bars show its 12-month actionable ending probability among that method’s eligible episodes. These have different denominators. * marks fewer than 30 observed actionable endings for the method in that province.

IUD

Other modern

A discrete-time multinomial episode-month model distinguishes continuation, actionable ending, and other competing ending. Model 1 includes month of method use and method mix plus province. Model 2 additionally includes age and parity at method start, marital status, education, wealth, residence, and start cohort. Common-mix predictions set each episode in the pooled analytic sample to each province in turn, then average; this makes province comparisons at the same method and population composition. Design-aware intervals and pairwise tests are reported in the updated province section below; small cells remain exploratory.

Illustrative 2026 mCPR and improved continuation

The 2026 denominator is 26,146,676.5 projected women aged 15–49 from the UN World Population Prospects 2024 medium variant. The survey 2024 all-women mCPR is carried to 2025 with a +0.4 percentage-point planning increment; 2026 scenarios use no further growth, UN +0.5 points, or Track20 +0.4 points.

AFT plus observed pathways Track20 growth (+0.4 pp) 9.9 10.3 2,598,897.9 26,146,676.5 10.7 2,796,108.6 298,359.2 346,933.9 1.1 11.8

The 2023–24 survey provides the 2024 all-women mCPR baseline. The illustrative 2025 current-user stock applies the Track20/FPET recent all-women increment of +0.4 percentage points to that baseline and uses projected 2025 women aged 15–49. The three 2026 cases then add no further growth, the UN modeled 2025–26 increment of +0.5 points, or another +0.4 points from the Track20/FPET recent trend. Only the increments—not the modelled prevalence levels—are transferred to the survey baseline. All cases use projected 2026 women aged 15–49 as the percentage-point denominator.

These are upper-bound accounting illustrations. The first-year approach counts endings of the original method, including women who may switch and remain modern users. The historical and AFT approaches condition on elapsed method use and use observed post-ending pathways to identify women outside modern use at year end. Avoiding an actionable ending is assumed to retain those otherwise nonmodern women in modern use; no intervention effectiveness is estimated.

Direct modern switching changes the gross user-loss illustration

Immediate modern-to-modern switching is an original-method ending but does not immediately remove a woman from the modern-use numerator. Later restarting can also restore modern use before year end.

AFT duration model with observed switching pathways

The AFT estimates of original-method endings are combined with observed switching and restarting patterns. IUD modeling has limited precision and uses an other-modern proxy.
